LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at Testimony 4: "Her parting gift is this letter, which she tucked safely away in her left breast pocket." This suggests Ms. Crane placed the letter in her left breast pocket as she was dying.

However, examining the evidence, we have:
- Evidence 3: "Letter" - A letter Kay was carrying. It is addressed to her.
- Evidence 13: "Victim's Letter" - A letter that appears to have been written to the victim by Kay.

The contradiction arises because if Ms. Crane placed the letter in her left breast pocket as her "dying breath" act, then Kay could not have been carrying that same letter (Evidence 3), as it would have been with the victim's body.

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

Rule 2 states: "If XXX is found in the left breast pocket then XXX was placed there while the wearer was upright."

**Step 3:** Apply the rule to reveal the contradiction

According to Rule 2, if the letter was found in the left breast pocket (as stated in Testimony 4), it must have been placed there while Ms. Crane was upright. However, Testimony 3 states "with her dying breath," which implies she was not upright when placing the letter. Additionally, if the letter was placed in the victim's pocket at death, it could not simultaneously be the letter that Kay was carrying (Evidence 3).

{"evidence": 3, "testimony": 4}

- Bad: ```json {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3} ```

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at Atmey's testimonies, he consistently describes being attacked from the front while facing the thief. Specifically:
- Testimony 2: "the thief grabbed a weapon from the side and... rendered me senseless"
- Testimony 5: "I put my back to the wall to fight, but the thief's blow landed upon my third eye"

However, the Shichishito (Evidence 6) states: "DeMasque knocked Atmey out during the crime w/ a blow to the back of the head."

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

Rule 2 applies here: "If XXX is behind YYY then YYY is in front of XXX"

**Step 3:** Explain how Rule 2 reveals the contradiction

According to Rule 2, if the blow came from behind Atmey's head (as stated in Evidence 6), then Atmey must have been facing away from the attacker. However, Atmey's testimonies consistently describe facing the thief - either putting his "back to the wall" (implying he was facing outward toward the room) or describing the thief attacking from the front/side. A blow to the back of the head is only possible if the attacker was behind Atmey, which contradicts Atmey's claim that he was facing the thief.

**Step 4:** Not applicable - Rule 2 applies

**Step 5:** {"evidence": 6, "testimony": 5}

- Testimony 8: "The victim ran to the left, and you gave chase!"
- Testimony 4: "I saw him: a spiky-haired man attacking a woman with long hair!"

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Rule 1 applies here: "If XXX is to the left of YYY then YYY is to the right of XXX"

This rule establishes that left and right are mutually exclusive directions. If the victim ran to the left (as White claims), then by definition they did not run to the right (as May claims). The two testimonies present opposite directional movements that cannot both be true simultaneously.

Not applicable - Rule 1 directly applies to this contradiction.

**Step 3:** **If applicable rules exist,** explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed rules apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
- Write your analysis first. Then the VERY LAST line must be exactly one JSON object like: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- The final line must contain only the JSON object with no leading or trailing text or whitespace (no labels, no markdown, no code fences). The response must end immediately at the closing brace """}""".

Examples:
- Good: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- Bad: **Step 5:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- Bad: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3} extra
- Bad: ```json {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3} ```

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at the testimonies, Testimony 3 states: "The only exit from the storeroom is the hatch that leads to the viewing platform." This suggests that to leave the storeroom, one must go up to the viewing platform.

However, Evidence 17 (Ms. Hart's Picture) shows "The figure is in the storeroom, walking towards a ladder that goes upwards." This indicates there's a ladder in the storeroom that goes up, which would be an alternative exit route from the storeroom.

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

Rule 5 states: "If XXX is on the ladder then XXX is ascending from the storeroom to the viewing platform." This rule confirms that the ladder in the storeroroom leads to the viewing platform, which would be consistent with Testimony 3.

However, the contradiction lies in the claim that the hatch is the "only exit" from the storeroom, when the picture clearly shows a ladder that also provides an exit route from the storeroom to the viewing platform.

**Step 3:** The rules don't directly reveal the contradiction, but they help establish the spatial relationship. Rule 5 shows the ladder is indeed a valid path from storeroom to viewing platform, making Testimony 3's claim of "only exit" factually incorrect.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning shows that while both the hatch and the ladder lead to the viewing platform, Testimony 3 incorrectly claims the hatch is the "only exit" from the storeroom, when the ladder (shown in Evidence 17) provides another exit route.

{"evidence": 17, "testimony": 3}

**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Lang's testimony claims that Gravelle and Shaun hid President Wang's body "inside that monster costume over there" (Testimony 3), and that "there's dirt inside the costume" (Testimony 4). However, the Crime Scene Notes (Evidence 5) state that "the body lying face-up on top of the dirt, and yet there's no dirt on the front of the body." This creates a spatial contradiction - if the body was hidden inside the monster costume (which would presumably cover the entire body), there should be dirt on the front of the body from being inside the costume with dirt. Instead, the evidence shows the body was on top of the dirt with no dirt on its front.

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules from the list above apply to this contradiction

Rule 2 states: "If XXX is inside YYY then YYY contains XXX." This rule applies here as Lang claims the body (XXX) was inside the monster costume (YYY), which would mean the costume contains the body.

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Rule 2 reveals the contradiction: If the body was inside the monster costume (as per Testimony 3), then the costume would contain the body. Given that there's dirt inside the costume (Testimony 4), the body should have dirt on its front from being contained within the dirty costume. However, the Crime Scene Notes (Evidence 5) show the body was on top of the dirt with no dirt on its front, which contradicts the spatial relationship implied by being inside the dirty costume.

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules from the list above apply to this contradiction

None of the generated spatial reasoning rules (Rules 1-10) directly apply to this contradiction. These rules primarily deal with directional relationships (north/south, left/right, above/below, inside/outside, adjacent to) and containment relationships, but do not address the spatial relationship of an object moving from one person's clothing to another's during a struggle.

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Not applicable - no rules from the list apply to this specific contradiction.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The spatial reasoning contradiction lies in the implausibility of the button's location. For the button to be ripped from Corrida's Jammin' Ninja costume (where it would naturally fall to the ground or remain with the victim) and then somehow end up inside Engarde's hakama (a traditional Japanese divided skirt), there would need to be direct physical contact between the two individuals. However, the testimony presents this as evidence of guilt without explaining how the button could have physically transferred from the victim's costume to the defendant's clothing during the alleged struggle.

This creates a spatial contradiction because if Pearl was stuck on the Inner Temple side (Testimony 0) and was in the spare prep room when the bridge was fixed (Testimony 2), then Godot could not have been arriving at the Inner Temple for the first time at that exact moment, as he would have needed to already be on the Inner Temple side to find Pearl in the spare prep room.

However, the spatial inconsistency is clear: the forehead and right temple are distinct anatomical locations that cannot both be correct for a single gunshot wound.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning

The testimony describes the bullet hitting "square in the forehead" (center of the forehead), while the autopsy report places the wound at the "right temple" (side of the head). These are mutually exclusive locations for a single bullet wound, creating a direct contradiction in the spatial description of the fatal injury.

**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, we have a sequence of events:
- Maya was hit over the head with the staff (Testimony 0)
- Maya dodged Elise's next attack and stole her weapon (Testimony 1)
- Elise was on the defensive with her back to the stone lantern (Testimony 2)

- Elise's autopsy report (Evidence 11) states the cause of death was "loss of blood from stab in back" and "Body fell 10 ft. after death"

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

Looking at the spatial rules:
- Rule 1: If XXX is stabbed in the back then XXX must be facing away from the attacker
- Rule 3: If XXX collapses at YYY then XXX must have been standing at YYY when struck
- Rule 8: If XXX falls 10 feet then XXX must have been 10 feet above ground level

**Step 3:** Apply the relevant rules to reveal the contradiction

According to Testimony 2, Elise had her "back to the stone lantern" when Maya stabbed her. This aligns with Rule 1 - if Elise was stabbed in the back, she must have been facing away from Maya, which is consistent with having her back to the stone lantern.

However, there's a critical spatial contradiction: Evidence 11 states the body "fell 10 ft. after death." According to Rule 8, this means Elise must have been 10 feet above ground level when struck. But the stone lantern (Evidence 20) is described as "A lantern from the Inner Temple garden" - there's no indication it was 10 feet tall or that Elise was elevated when stabbed. The testimonies describe a ground-level fight in the courtyard, with no mention of any elevation.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning contradiction

The testimonies describe a fight occurring at ground level in the courtyard, with Elise backing against a stone lantern. However, the autopsy report requires Elise to have been 10 feet above ground level when stabbed to account for the 10-foot fall. These two pieces of information are spatially incompatible.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at the evidence and testimonies, there's a clear spatial contradiction involving Olga's location. Evidence 4 (Wright's Cell Phone) states it was "used by the defendant to notify the police from the restaurant's first floor." However, Olga's testimony 5 claims "I didn't let him out of my sight until the cops got there!" This creates a contradiction because if Olga was watching Wright continuously in the basement (as she claims), she couldn't have seen him go to the first floor to use his phone.

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules from the list above apply to this contradiction

Several rules apply to this contradiction:
- Rule 2: If XXX claims to be in the basement the whole time then XXX cannot be on the first floor
- Rule 3: If XXX is found on the first floor then XXX was not in the basement the whole time
- Rule 4: If XXX is used to notify police from the first floor then XXX was on the first floor
- Rule 6: If XXX is observed in the basement then XXX cannot simultaneously be observed on the first floor
- Rule 7: If XXX is described as being in the basement during the crime then XXX cannot have left the basement unnoticed
- Rule 8: If XXX is stated to have been watched continuously then XXX's location cannot change without observation

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Rules 4, 7, and 8 directly reveal the contradiction:
- Rule 4: Wright's cell phone was used to notify police from the first floor, which means Wright was on the first floor
- Rule 7: Olga claims Wright was in the basement during the crime and she didn't let him out of her sight
- Rule 8: Olga states she watched Wright continuously, which means his location couldn't change without her observation

These rules show that Olga's testimony that she "didn't let him out of my sight" contradicts the evidence that Wright was on the first floor to use his phone, since the basement is two floors below ground level (as established by the story).

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
- Write your analysis first. Then the VERY LAST line must be exactly one JSON object like: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- The final line must contain only the JSON object with no leading or trailing text or whitespace (no labels, no markdown, no code fences). The response must end immediately at the closing brace """}""".

- Good: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- Bad: **Step 5:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- Bad: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3} extra
- Bad: ```json {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3} ```

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at the testimonies, Olga states in Testimony 3: "The one who was winning... dah, it was victim!" This suggests Shadi Smith (the victim) was winning the poker game.

However, examining Evidence 6 (Chip Photo), we see:
- Near side (defendant's side): 4 red small chips (100 points each = 400 points) + 1 gray big chip (1,000 points) = 1,400 points
- Far side (victim's side): 2 red small chips (200 points) + 9 gray big chips (9,000 points) = 9,200 points

But this contradicts the initial chip distribution from Testimony 0: "The game began with 3,500 point in chips for each man." The victim has 9,200 points, which is significantly more than the starting 3,500, while the defendant has only 1,400 points, which is much less than the starting amount.

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

Looking at the spatial reasoning rules, Rule 5 states: "If XXX is on the near side of the table then XXX belongs to the nearest player" and Rule 6 states: "If XXX is on the far side of the table then XXX belongs to the opposite player." These rules help us correctly identify which chips belong to which player based on their position on the table.

**Step 3:** Apply the rules to reveal the contradiction

Using Rules 5 and 6, we can confirm that the chips on the near side belong to the defendant (Wright) and the chips on the far side belong to the victim (Smith). The spatial arrangement shows the victim has 9,200 points worth of chips, which contradicts Olga's testimony that the victim was "winning" - because if the victim was truly winning, he should have more chips than he started with, but the defendant should have correspondingly fewer chips. Instead, the defendant has only 1,400 points, which is 2,100 points less than the starting amount, while the victim has 5,700 points more than the starting amount. This imbalance suggests the testimony about who was winning may be incorrect.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning shows that the chip distribution in Evidence 6 contradicts the claim in Testimony 3 about who was winning, as the victim has accumulated far more chips than possible through normal play.

This creates a direct contradiction - Starbuck claims he was taking Clay away from danger, but the body was found in a location that would require moving TOWARD the boarding lounge, not away from it.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning shows that Starbuck's claim of "getting us out of the rocket" contradicts the evidence placing Clay's body in the boarding lounge, which is connected to Launch Pad 1 rather than being a location one would escape TO.

## CONSTRAINT:

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 2:** **Check if any generated rules** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable rules exist,** explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed rules apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
- Write your analysis first. Then the VERY LAST line must be exactly one JSON object like: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- The final line must contain only the JSON object with no leading or trailing text or whitespace (no labels, no markdown, no code fences). The response must end immediately at the closing brace """}""".

Examples:
- Good: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- Bad: **Step 5:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}
- Bad: {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3} extra
- Bad: ```json {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3} ```

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at the testimonies:
- Testimony 1: The victim was hit from behind and fell over, right into the lantern
- Testimony 3: The victim was struck twice

Looking at the evidence:
- Evidence 6 (Photo of Victim): Shows the victim lying face up in the broken lantern
- Evidence 14 (Autopsy Report): States Gloomsbury's head shows signs of being hit twice

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

The contradiction here is not about spatial relationships (inside, left, right, above, below, behind, front) but about the sequence and nature of the attack. The testimonies describe the victim being hit from behind and falling into the lantern, while the evidence shows the victim face up in the lantern with two head injuries.

**Step 3:** Since this is not a spatial contradiction, none of the listed rules apply

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The spatial contradiction lies between Testimony 1 and Evidence 6. Testimony 1 claims the victim was hit from behind and "fell over, right into the lantern." However, Evidence 6 (the photo) shows the victim lying face up in the lantern. If someone is hit from behind and falls forward into a lantern, they would typically land face down, not face up. This spatial inconsistency suggests the testimony about the direction of the attack may be inaccurate.

**Step 5:** {"evidence": 6, "testimony": 1}

**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

However, the evidence shows that the victim was shot at point-blank range with gunpowder burns (Evidence 9), and the bullet path went from abdomen to shoulder (Evidence 6), indicating an upward trajectory.

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

Rule 1: "If XXX is shot from above then XXX is below the shooter" - This would apply if the shooting was indeed from above.
Rule 5: "If XXX is shot through the abdomen and shoulder then the bullet path is upward" - This indicates the bullet traveled upward through the body.

Rule 2: "If XXX is shot point-blank then the shooter is within arm's reach of XXX" - This also confirms close proximity.

**Step 3:** Apply the rules to reveal the contradiction

The contradiction lies between Testimony 2 (shot from on high) and the physical evidence. If the victim was shot from above (Rule 1), the bullet path should be downward. However, Evidence 6 shows the bullet traveled upward from abdomen to shoulder (Rule 5), which is physically impossible if the shooter was positioned above the victim. Additionally, the gunpowder burns (Evidence 9) indicate point-blank range (Rule 6), which would be difficult to achieve if the shooter was positioned significantly above the victim.

**Step 4:** Spatial reasoning explanation

The spatial arrangement described in the testimony (shooter above victim) contradicts the physical evidence of an upward bullet trajectory. When shooting from above, gravity would cause the bullet to travel downward through the body, not upward from abdomen to shoulder. The upward trajectory can only occur if the shooter was positioned below the victim's shoulder level, shooting upward.

Rule 10 also supports this contradiction by stating that if someone falls near the workbench, they are not on the operating table, which again conflicts with the photographic evidence showing Metis on the operating table.

- For the thief to strike Atmey's forehead/third eye, the thief must be in front of Atmey
- However, Testimony 2 claims the weapon came "from the side," which contradicts this frontal positioning

**Step 3:** Explain how Rule 2 reveals the contradiction

Rule 2 establishes the directional relationship between objects. If Atmey's back is against the wall (wall behind Atmey), then the only direction the thief could be is in front of Atmey. The claim that the weapon came "from the side" is spatially impossible in this configuration, as there would be no "side" access when one's back is against a wall and the attacker is in front.

- Evidence 11: "Elise's Autopsy Report" states "Cause: loss of blood from stab in back" - This indicates Elise was stabbed in the back

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** Rule 1 applies: "If XXX is stabbed in the back then XXX must be facing away from the attacker"

According to Testimony 2, Elise had her "back to the stone lantern" when stabbed. This means she was facing AWAY from the stone lantern. However, if Maya stabbed Elise in the back (as stated in the autopsy report), then Elise must have been facing AWAY from Maya. This creates a spatial contradiction because if Elise's back was to the stone lantern, and she was stabbed in the back, then Maya would have had to be at the stone lantern stabbing Elise from that direction. But this contradicts the testimony that Elise was "on the defensive" facing Maya.

**Step 4:** The contradiction is revealed by applying Rule 1. If Elise was stabbed in the back (autopsy report), she must have been facing away from her attacker. However, Testimony 2 states she had her "back to the stone lantern," which would mean she was facing toward the open area where Maya would be. This creates an impossible spatial arrangement where Elise is simultaneously facing her attacker (Maya) while being stabbed in the back.

**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

- Evidence 18 (Lighter) was found with Athena's prints on it in the victim's blood
- Evidence 14 (Terran's Autopsy Report) states the victim was discovered in the third-floor boarding lounge in the main building
- Evidence 19 (Emergency Ladder) was lowered from the fourth-floor robotics lab and used for evacuation

- Rule 1: If XXX is on the third-floor boarding lounge then XXX is inside the main building
- Rule 5: If XXX is at launch pad 1 then XXX is outside the main building
- Rule 9: If XXX escaped via the emergency ladder then XXX did not use the launch pad 1 door
- Rule 10: If XXX is in the main building then XXX is not at launch pad 1

**Step 3:** Apply the rules to reveal the contradiction

The key contradiction lies in the escape route. Fulbright claims Athena is the culprit based on the lighter evidence, but the spatial evidence shows:

- The victim was found in the third-floor boarding lounge (inside the main building - Rule 1)
- The emergency ladder was used from the fourth-floor robotics lab (Rule 3)
- If someone used the emergency ladder, they did not use the launch pad 1 door (Rule 9)

However, the lighter has Athena's prints AND Clay Terran's fingerprints and blood on it. This suggests the lighter was at the crime scene (third-floor boarding lounge), but the spatial rules indicate that if Athena escaped via the emergency ladder (from fourth-floor robotics lab), she would have had to be in the robotics lab, not the boarding lounge where the victim was found.

**Step 4:** The contradiction is between the location where the lighter was found (implied to be at the crime scene in the boarding lounge) and the escape route via the emergency ladder from the robotics lab. The lighter being at the crime scene contradicts the spatial impossibility of being in both the boarding lounge (where the victim was found) and escaping via the robotics lab ladder.

- The body was found inside the elevator, not in the lounge itself
- The elevator is a separate enclosed space from the lounge
- For the body to be in the elevator, it must have been moved there from somewhere else

**Step 2:** Check if any generated rules apply to this contradiction

Rule 1 applies: "If XXX is inside the elevator at time T then XXX cannot be in the lounge at time T"

**Step 3:** Explain how Rule 1 reveals the contradiction

Rule 1 directly contradicts Franziska's testimony. If Akbey Hicks (the victim) was found inside the elevator at 6:15 AM, then by Rule 1, he could not have been in the lounge at that same time. This means Franziska's claim that "the only person in this lounge the entire time was you, Miles Edgeworth" is false because the victim's body was actually in the elevator, not in the lounge.
